- **File layout**: `theme.css` slimmed from 383 → 109 lines — the legacy component classes (`.dr-section-brief-btn`, `.dr-ghost-block`, `.dr-ghost-block-kind`, `.cap-bar`, `.bg-grid`, `.phase-step-line`, `.uppercase-label`) stay; tokens / reset / body / type utilities / markdown / scrollbars / focus / motion / pulse keyframes / selection now live in the new `tokens.css` + `base.css`. `theme.css` will drain spec-by-spec across Ship 2.
- **`index.html`**: Google Fonts link swapped (`Geist`+`JetBrains Mono` → `IBM Plex Sans`+`IBM Plex Serif`); `<link rel="stylesheet" href="theme.css">` replaced with `tokens.css` + `base.css` + `theme.css` (order matters — tokens first, base second, legacy last); new `<script type="text/babel" src="icons.jsx">` loaded ahead of `shared.jsx`.

### Added

- New file `src/dual_research/ui/static/tokens.css` — authoritative token sheet, copied verbatim from the Claude Design brief. Every component reads from these; no hex codes anywhere in components.
- New file `src/dual_research/ui/static/base.css` — body, type utilities (`.mono` / `.num` / `.serif` / `.t-*`), focus ring, reduced-motion contract, animations (`caret-blink`, `pulse-*`, `seg-in`, `dr-flash`, `dr-modal-in`), markdown rendering (`.md`). Copied verbatim from the brief.
- New file `src/dual_research/ui/static/icons.jsx` — MDI dictionary (~60 icons as inline path data) + `<Mdi>` React component. Copied verbatim from the brief.

### Tests

- 725 baseline pytest green (no Python changes; pure asset shuffle).
- Preview-verified on `localhost:6173` against the partner-vetting run (`3a4a`) — all surfaces render in both themes, ReconcileChip `unverified` state intact, GhostedAnnotation / RepairChip / cost chips functional, 88 MDI SVGs rendering on run-detail, zero console errors.

## [0.47.1] — 2026-05-17

### Fixed

- **Consumption tab now shows the *effective* input Claude actually saw, including cache reads and cache writes.** Pre-fix the per-turn bars and "User prompt: Brief" sub-bars sized themselves on the provider's raw `input_tokens` field, which for Claude only covers the *uncached* suffix after the `CACHE_BREAKPOINT` marker. The cached prefix (brief + Phase-1 drafts + agreed plan, depending on the phase) rides in via `cache_creation_input_tokens` on the first turn and `cache_read_input_tokens` thereafter — neither was being counted in the Consumption-tab display, so P0 Preflight showed `Claude: 400t in` vs `GPT: 59.6kt in` even though both agents received the same brief. The mismatch widened with phase round depth (Round 1 ~120× off) and shrank back as fresh history accumulated past the cache breakpoint (Round 2+ ~7× off). The bug only affected the display layer — the orchestrator was sending identical content to both agents the whole time, and cost reconciliation continued to work against the per-bucket numbers correctly. New `effectiveTokensIn(usage)` helper in `run-detail.jsx` sums `in + cacheRead + cacheWrite`; `computeConsumptionScale`, `ConsumptionCard`, and `TokenBar` all read from it now. Headline + tooltip on each card surface the breakdown (`60.0kt in · 1.2kt out · 50.0kt cached` + hover) so the cached vs fresh split stays auditable. Cost columns + ProviderBilledLine + ReconcileChip unchanged — those continue to use the per-bucket fields against the per-bucket pricing.

## [0.47.0] — 2026-05-17

### Added

- **`reconcile-costs --source {local,supabase}` flag** ([spec 0049](specs/0049-reconcile-from-supabase.md)) — new `gather_supabase_totals(client, *, start_date, end_date)` in `audit/reconcile.py` queries the hosted `runs` table (where runs persist via spec 0020) and produces the same `LocalTotals` shape `gather_local_totals` produces from disk. Default remains `local` (backward compatible); `--source supabase` requires Supabase creds in env. SQL-side prefilter on the indexed `created_at` column with a Python-side `metrics.started_at` check for canonical bucket attribution. `reconcile_day` / `reconcile_range` gain an optional `local_totals` kwarg so callers can precompute totals from any source once over a range.
- **GitHub Actions daily cron re-enabled** — `.github/workflows/reconcile-costs.yml` `schedule: "0 2 * * *"` line restored; command updated to `reconcile-costs --since-yesterday --source supabase --push --tolerance 1.0`. The CI runner's empty local `runs/` directory no longer matters; the cron reads from Supabase (where runs actually live). Daily snapshots upserted into the `reconcile_results` table for the run-detail verification chip to consume.

### Changed

- **`reconcile-costs` CLI does not warn about missing `runs/` when `--source supabase`** — `runs/` is unused in that mode; the warning was only meaningful for `--source local`.

### Added (tests)

- `tests/audit/test_reconcile_supabase_source.py` — 6 new tests cover `gather_supabase_totals`'s shape parity with `gather_local_totals`, SQL prefilter contract, window-boundary handling, missing-metrics tolerance, pricing-version collection. 725 total green (was 719).

## [0.46.1] — 2026-05-17

### Fixed

- **`reconcile-costs` no longer exits 2 when `runs/` is missing.** The 0.46.0 CLI bailed early on a missing `runs/` directory, which broke the GitHub Actions cron (CI runners check out a clean repo where `runs/` is gitignored and absent). The CLI now logs a warning and proceeds with empty local totals — `gather_local_totals` already handled the missing-dir case correctly; only the CLI's early exit was overzealous. New regression test `tests/audit/test_reconcile_cli_missing_runs.py` locks the behaviour. 719 total green (was 718).

### Changed

- **Disabled the daily reconcile-costs cron schedule (`.github/workflows/reconcile-costs.yml`).** The `workflow_dispatch` trigger remains so manual runs work, but the daily 02:00 UTC schedule is commented out. Reason: CI runners have no local `runs/` directory, so the cron would reconcile `$0 local vs $X billed` daily and report spurious drift — eroding trust in the run-detail verification chip. Re-enable in a follow-up spec that teaches `reconcile-costs` to read run-cost data from Supabase (where the actual run metrics live in hosted mode).

## [0.46.0] — 2026-05-17

### Added

- **Always-on cost verification against provider invoices** ([spec 0048](specs/0048-cost-reconciliation-and-pricing-version.md) C1) — new `dual-research reconcile-costs` CLI subcommand pulls daily cost aggregates from the OpenAI Organization Costs API and the Anthropic Admin Cost Report API, joins to local `metrics.json` totals over the same UTC date range, computes per-(provider, model) deltas, and writes a `ReconcileReport` to `reconcile/<date>.json`. Five `verification_status` states (`verified` / `drift` / `partial` / `unverified` / `awaiting_provider_data`) honestly reflect what was checked. **Each provider's admin key is independently optional**: set `OPENAI_ADMIN_KEY` and/or `ANTHROPIC_ADMIN_KEY` in env (plus optional `OPENAI_PROJECT_ID` / `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` for scoping); a missing key surfaces as `providers_skipped`, never crashes. CLI modes: `--day` · `--from/--to` · `--all` · `--run RUN_ID` · `--since-yesterday`. Output: human-readable text (default) or `--format json`. `--push` upserts each report into the new Supabase `reconcile_results` table (hosted-mode storage); `--write-snapshots` (default true) persists `reconcile/<date>.json` locally. Exit code 0 = within tolerance, 1 = any flagged row (CI-friendly).
- **Run-detail verification chip** ([spec 0048](specs/0048-cost-reconciliation-and-pricing-version.md) D13) — new `<ReconcileChip>` in the run-detail header reads `/api/reconcile/<date>` and renders one of five visual states: `✓ verified $X.XX`, `⚠ Δ $Y.YY (billed $Z.ZZ)`, `local $X.XX · ✓ OpenAI · ⚠ Anthropic`, `local $X.XX · unverified`, `local $X.XX · awaiting provider data`. Hover tooltip lists checked providers, skipped reasons, exact local vs billed totals, and the snapshot timestamp.
- **Consumption-tab "provider-billed" annotation** ([spec 0048](specs/0048-cost-reconciliation-and-pricing-version.md) D14) — each `ConsumptionCard` whose `(provider, model_id)` matches a per-model delta on the day's `ReconcileReport` shows a `Provider-billed: $X · Δ $Y (Z%)` line under the costs cluster. Flagged rows (delta exceeds tolerance) get a warn-tinted ⚠.
- **`/api/reconcile/{date}` server endpoint** — returns the latest `ReconcileReport` for the date; 404 when no snapshot exists. Local mode reads from `<project>/reconcile/<date>.json`; hosted mode reads from the Supabase `reconcile_results` table. `/api/reconcile/index` lists available dates.
- **GitHub Actions daily cron** — `.github/workflows/reconcile-costs.yml` runs `reconcile-costs --since-yesterday --push` at 02:00 UTC daily. Manual `workflow_dispatch` available. Provider keys + Supabase creds via repository secrets.
- **Supabase `reconcile_results` table** — `supabase/migrations/0004_reconcile_results.sql`: `(date PK, payload JSONB, checked_at TIMESTAMPTZ)` with index on `checked_at DESC`. Re-running upserts so the latest snapshot wins.
- **`pricing_version` snapshot on `metrics.json`** ([spec 0048](specs/0048-cost-reconciliation-and-pricing-version.md) F8) — new `PRICING_VERSION` constant in `src/dual_research/agents/pricing.py` (initial value `"2026-05-17"`, human-bumped when any `ModelPricing` entry changes). `Metrics.to_json()` records it on every save; `Metrics.load()` reads back tolerantly (defaults to `""` on pre-0048 files). A snapshot regression test (`tests/agents/test_pricing_version.py::test_version_tracks_table`) hashes the `PRICING` dict so a rate-table edit can't ship without a matching version bump.

### Changed

- **`recompute-costs` stamps the rewritten `metrics.json` with the live `PRICING_VERSION`** and surfaces the before/after transition on the CLI per-run diff line (`↳ pricing table: (unknown) → 2026-05-17`). `RecomputeReport` gains `pricing_version_before` / `_after` fields. Lets reconcile distinguish rate-table mismatches from accounting drift.

### Added (tests)

- 9 new test files / ~45 new tests across `tests/agents/`, `tests/persistence/`, `tests/audit/`. Reconcile module tested against the real OpenAI cost-report response (captured 2026-05-17 against the dual-search project, saved as `tests/audit/fixtures/openai_cost_report_2026_05_15_to_17.json`) and against a canonical Anthropic docs-shape fixture (revalidated when an admin key becomes available). Five-state status machine pure-function tests; persistence round-trip; CLI orchestration with `httpx.MockTransport`; pricing-version snapshot regression.

## [0.45.0] — 2026-05-17

### Fixed

- **Run-detail page no longer crashes on `drafter=null + completed` runs** ([spec 0047](specs/0047-run-detail-resilience.md) F1) — five historical local runs (anything killed before Phase 3 but marked complete by an older orchestrator path) rendered a blank page with React error `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'name')`. `ArtifactHeader`'s `kind === 'doc'` and `kind === 'doc-live'` branches now guard `meta` defensively (matching the pattern already established in `DocumentModal`); `buildLiveTimeline` skips the `doc-final` push entirely when `run.drafter` is null, so the artifact strip just doesn't include a "Final document by null" card for those runs.
- **Finalize-path None-Phase-2 + deadlocked combination** ([spec 0047](specs/0047-run-detail-resilience.md) F2) — the resume-with-`phase2_outcome=None` path had test coverage (spec 0036) for the APPROVED branch but not for the DEADLOCKED branch. New regression test in `tests/orchestrator/test_emit_final_resume.py` exercises the deadlock appendix while Phase 2 is None to lock the guard against future regressions. Sweep of `finalize.py` confirmed no remaining unguarded `phase{N}_outcome.<attr>` accesses on `Foo | None` parameters.

### Changed

- **Phase 4 (and Phase 2) protocol-repair turns appear as their own Consumption-tab cards** ([spec 0047](specs/0047-run-detail-resilience.md) F5) — per-turn key on the wire gains a `_repair` suffix for sibling labels (`phase4-r1-claude-repair`, `phase2-r4-gpt-hashdrift-repair`). Pre-spec the aggregator's `_on_turn_ended` collided original + repair onto the same `phase4_round1_claude` key and the per-turn dict overwrite hid the repair cost; agent-level totals were correct but the Consumption tab under-reported. The new behaviour matches the suffix convention already used by `_on_turn_inputs` and `_on_turn_searches` for the input bundle and search audit. Consumption-tab `buildConsumptionRows` regex extended to capture the `Repair` suffix; repair rows sort immediately after their parent with a slightly muted background + small `repair` chip in the label cell. Timeline `StatsChips` gains a `+repair` discoverability chip on the parent turn so the user knows to look at the Consumption tab for the per-call breakdown. Agent-level rollups unchanged (still sum every event — matches the billing aggregate). 665 total green (was 659).

## [0.44.0] — 2026-05-17

### Added / Changed

- **Critique panel + Summary + Consumption rework + design unification** ([spec 0046](specs/0046-critique-summary-consumption-rework.md)) — 10 visual decisions across the three surfaces specs 0042/0043/0044 fed honest data into. **(1) Critique pane header rewrite.** Three primary buttons on the left (`Phase 2 Negotiate` / `Phase 4 Review` / `∑ Summary`) ARE the navigation; the count cluster (`introduced · open · resolved · ⚠ drift`) sits right-aligned. Pre-spec the header led with `Critique · N introduced` and the buttons sat in the toolbar below; spec 0046 puts the navigation at the visual anchor. **(2) Per-phase context-aware filter chips.** `filterChipsFor(phase)` walks the spec-0042 `PHASE_CHIP_ALLOWLIST`; Phase 2 active → `[All | Questions | Disagreements | Claims]`; Phase 4 active → `[All | Issues | Comments | Disagreements]`. Zero-count chips for kinds the phase doesn't have are suppressed. Switching phase auto-resets the filter if the previous selection isn't allowed in the new phase. **(3) Human-readable card headlines.** New shared `CardHeadline` component renders `{Kind} {Public ID} · {status} · {body}` for Question / Disagreement / Issue / Comment cards. The cryptic `**C-1** — \`open\` — body` prefix is stripped via a new `stripMarkdown` helper; the round range + internal ID (`I-c-r1-01`) move to the expanded body. **(4) `GhostedAnnotation` wired into every critique card.** New `findLedgerEntry(run, phaseId, itemId)` resolves a card to its spec-0043 ledger entry; when `entry.ghostedRounds > 0` the headline renders a small `⚠ ghosted Nr` badge with a tooltip explaining the semantic. **(5) Summary tab redesign.** Replaces the single 11-column table where most cells were empty with one table per kind the phase emits. Columns: `Round | Claude raised | Claude {closed} | GPT raised | GPT {closed} | Open`. Per-kind, per-model — the user's "models are not there" complaint addressed. Empty rounds + kinds excluded; comments drop the resolved/open columns (stateless). **(6) Consumption single-row card with inline expand.** Each phase-round is one `<article>` card; expand renders the per-piece breakdowns INSIDE the same card (not as a separate full-width row below). Visual flow stays linear top-to-bottom. **(7) Drop "not used in this turn".** Same fix spec 0045 D3 applied to the input full-view; empty pieces simply don't render. **(8) Web-search cluster restructure.** Replaces the confusing `web searches: N · of which web search: $X` wording with a clean two-line `Tokens: $A · Web search: $B · Total: $T` + `Searches: N · Queries: M` cluster (counts line hidden when zero). **(9) Uniform `PaneButton` component.** New shared `PaneButton` + `PaneButtonGroup` replaces three disparate toggle/tab/filter styles across the Critique header, filter chips, and Timeline pane's `Conversation` / `Consumption` segmented control. One design language; differences are semantic (active / hover / disabled), not cosmetic. **(10) No backend changes.** Pure JSX + CSS refactor over the wire data shipped in specs 0042/0043/0044. **2 new regression-guard tests** in `tests/ui/test_aggregator_ledger.py` lock the wire-format contract D4 depends on (ledger entries carry `ghosted_rounds`; entry ids match parsed-item ids so `findLedgerEntry` resolves). 659 total green (was 657).

## [0.43.0] — 2026-05-17

### Changed

- **Full-view shell standardisation + model pill layout** ([spec 0045](specs/0045-full-view-shell-and-model-pills.md)) — layout-only chrome cleanup across every full-view modal and the timeline-header agent pills. **(1) Canonical tab order.** Every full-view modal (`DocumentModal`, `NegotiateReviewModal`, `DraftReviewModal`, `InputBriefModal`, `PreflightResponseModal`) now builds its tabs list and sorts through a shared `TABS_CANON = ['content', 'input', 'webSearch', 'sources', 'files']` so the eye doesn't have to re-orient between modals. The side-by-side sub-tab strips (`Original | Input | Web Search`) follow the same order. **(2) Hide-empty tabs.** Web Search tabs are omitted when the per-turn summary has zero queries and zero consulted sources (the hallucination-⚠ exception only fires when there are actual searches to flag); Sources / Files tabs in the brief modal are omitted when the bucket is empty. Tab labels no longer carry counts — the body header carries the count instead. **(3) Input full-view drops "not used in this turn" rows.** Per-turn input bundles render only the pieces the orchestrator actually inlined; the wire format still carries empty-string entries for the full piece vocabulary, but the frontend filters them out. **(4) "User prompt: Brief" rename + top-float.** The brief is labelled `User prompt: Brief` (it IS the user prompt — today's CLI has no separate `--prompt` field) and floats above the system template in the input full-view. **(5) Equal-width side-by-side columns.** `NegotiateReviewModal` was `1.5fr / 1fr` (left-biased); `DraftReviewModal` was `1fr / 1.3fr` (right-biased). Both now `1fr / 1fr` so the two panes read as parallel surfaces. **(6) Equal-width agent pills with identity / metrics zones.** The Claude and GPT pills in the timeline header share a hard-coded `min-width: 480px` (≈20% above the wider Claude pill's pre-spec width); internal layout is identity left-aligned (logo · provider · model), spacer, metrics right-aligned (tokens · cost · ● status). **2 new regression tests** in `tests/ui/test_aggregator_input_bundles.py` lock the wire-format contract the frontend filter depends on (persisted bundle preserves empty-string entries; synthesised Phase 0 bundle carries the brief text in `pieces.brief`). 657 total green (was 655). No backend behavioural changes; pure JSX + CSS refactor consuming existing wire shapes.

## [0.42.0] — 2026-05-17

### Added / Changed

- **Turn-input semantics + per-turn badge redesign + side-by-side framing** ([spec 0044](specs/0044-turn-input-semantics-and-badge-redesign.md)) — pulls the spec-0043 ledger up into the timeline + side-by-side modal so chip counts, sentiment, and the modal's left-pane match what the system actually tracks instead of what each agent self-reports.

  **(1) Per-turn badge redesign.** The `negotiating` / `reviewing` status pill is removed from `StatsChips` — the phase-section header already labels the phase, so the per-turn pill was noise. Count chips now render as explicit `+raised  −resolved` per kind (e.g. `+5 Q  −1`, `−3 prior Q`, `+10 Cl`) instead of a single number with a cryptic `⤴` glyph. Values come from the ledger: `raised` = entries with `raisedTurnKey === turnKey`; `resolved` = entries whose `statusHistory` includes a non-`open` transition with `turnKey === turnKey`. Zero-on-both-sides chips are omitted (quiet turns stay sparse).

  **(2) `✓ agreed` / `✓ approved` pill semantics fixed.** Previously the per-turn `agreed` pill fired on each agent's per-turn `STATUS: AGREED` self-report, which can fire mid-phase before the other side agrees. Spec 0044 ties it to the phase-wide convergence state via `isFinalConvergedTurn(item, run)`: only renders on the LAST turn of a phase whose ledger reports zero open blocking entries AND whose `phaseTimings[phase]` is populated. Phase 2 gates on questions + disagreements + claims; Phase 4 gates on issues — mirrors the orchestrator's convergence check.

  **(3) Side-by-side modal left pane gets phase-aware document tabs.** `NegotiateReviewModal` now renders a `NegotiateDocTabs` strip listing every document the agent actually had as input for the turn: **P2 R1** = `[Other's draft | Brief | Your draft]`; **P2 R≥2** = `[Other's prior turn | Other's draft | Brief | Your draft]`; **P4** = `[Current draft | Other's prior turn | Brief]`; **P1 plan-draft** = `[Brief]`. The default tab is the most-likely-relevant "thing being responded to" (first entry). The existing `Input` and `Web Search` sub-tabs stay (per-turn, not per-document). Right-pane review-item clicks still anchor against the first tab — switching tabs is a manual user action; jump-against-active-tab is a deferred enhancement.

  **(4) Right-pane empty-state copy is action-specific.** Distinguishes three cases that previously read identically: "no activity at all" → "raised no new items and closed no prior ones"; "only-closed" → "closed N {kinds} from prior rounds"; "raised-but-unanchored" → "raised N item(s) but none had quote/after anchors." Reads ledger deltas to pick the right copy.

  **(5) Click-to-highlight wired on `DraftReviewModal` (Phase 1 plan-draft).** Spec 0042 D1 extracted Phase 1 claims + open questions as structured items in `phase1_<agent>` ledger buckets, but the Phase 1 modal had no UI surface for them. Spec 0044 adds a `Phase1ItemStrip` above the draft body showing one chip per anchored item; click jumps the left brief pane to the matching block via `scrollAndFlash`. Reuses the same anchor-resolution logic as `NegotiateReviewModal`.

  **(6) `composeSentiment` ledger-aware.** Phase 2's "Standing:" line now reads `run.phaseLedgers[2]` for per-phase open counts (questions + disagreements + claims) instead of the agent's `OPEN_QUESTIONS:` / `BLOCKING_DISAGREEMENTS:` self-counters. Phase 4 similarly reads the ledger's open-issue count. Falls back to self-counters when the ledger isn't populated (legacy snapshots). The sentiment word picker stays status-driven.

  **3 new tests** in `tests/ui/test_aggregator_ledger.py` guarding the wire-format shape the frontend consumes (`statusHistory[].turnKey`, `raised_turn_key` field presence + >80% populated, `phaseTimings` populated on completed runs). 655 total green (was 652). No backend behavioural changes — pure consumption of spec 0043's wire surface.

### Deferred to future specs

- Per-card ghosted-N-rounds annotation in critique cards (`GhostedAnnotation` is defined in spec 0043 but not yet wired into `QuestionCard` / `IssueCard`) — spec 0046 visual rework.
- Critique panel header rework (P2/P4/Summary buttons + filter-chip relabeling + Summary tab redesign) — spec 0046.
- Full-view shell standardisation (tab order, hide-unused-sections, equal-width Original vs Draft columns, model pill equal-width + alignment + size) — spec 0045.
- Drift event drill-down UI — chip + tooltip remain v1 surface; deferred until drift becomes common enough to warrant.
- Jump-against-active-tab on the side-by-side modal (right-pane click currently anchors to the first tab; switching tabs doesn't rebind anchors).

## [0.41.0] — 2026-05-17

### Added

- **Authoritative cross-round ledger + standing-items input + conservative convergence** ([spec 0043](specs/0043-cross-round-ledger-and-conservative-convergence.md)) — the system now maintains a programmatic record of every claim / question / disagreement / issue / comment raised across each phase's rounds, derived from the existing parsed sections in each turn file. **No new agent output required** — the ledger is built by parsing what agents already write. Three changes that work together: **(1) Ledger package (`src/dual_research/ledger/`)** — `LedgerEntry` / `LedgerState` / `LedgerStatusTransition` / `LedgerDrift` dataclasses; `build_phase_ledger(session_dir, phase)` walks turn files chronologically and applies per-kind closure detection (questions: `## Answers to:` positional + verbatim-match; disagreements: D-N appearance in `## Resolved or non-blocking differences` / `## Final-surfaced disagreements`; claims: D-N suffix match against substantive-disagreements / resolved / final sections for escalation; issues: latest-round body-marker semantics; comments: terminal `noted`). Ghosted-rounds counter accumulates each round an open item received no addressing signal. **(2) `## Standing items from prior rounds` injected into R≥2 prompts** via `negotiation_turn_prompt` + `review_turn_prompt`. The orchestrator (`phase2.py` / `phase4.py`) builds the section per-agent before composing each round's prompt; items raised by the other agent surface first (grouped "what's still on them" vs "what you still hold"). Soft instruction — items left unaddressed surface as ghosted in the UI. Bounded at 30 entries / 3000 chars with a truncation footnote. **(3) Conservative convergence cross-check** — `is_plan_agreed` + `is_review_approved` accept an optional `ledger_open_count` parameter; convergence terminates only when both the agent self-counters AND the ledger open-set agree. Drift signal surfaced on `Run.drifts: list[LedgerDrift]` for end-of-phase mismatches between agent counters and ledger counts. **(4) Wire surface** — `Run.phase_ledgers: dict[int, list[LedgerEntry]]` keyed by phase (2, 4). UI binds `run.phaseLedgers[2|4]` as the canonical source for cross-round display. **(5) Kill-switch** — env flag `DR_LEDGER_MODE=legacy` falls back to self-counter-only convergence (skips ledger check; omits standing-items section). Default `enforce`. **(6) Frontend** — small `LedgerDriftChip` on the Critique pane header surfaces per-phase drift events with a tooltip breakdown; `GhostedAnnotation` component added (forward-compatible for spec 0046's visual rework). **33 new tests** across `tests/ledger/` (models, build transitions, prompt composer, kill-switch) + `tests/protocol/test_convergence_ledger.py` + `tests/ui/test_aggregator_ledger.py`. 652 total green (was 619). The wire-format additions (`Run.phase_ledgers`, `Run.drifts`) are foundational for spec 0046's Critique pane redesign; 0043 itself ships the data + a minimal drift indicator.

### Deferred to future specs

- Per-card ghosted-N-rounds annotations in the critique panel (spec 0046 visual rework will wire `GhostedAnnotation` into `QuestionCard` / `IssueCard` / etc.).
- Removing the `OPEN_QUESTIONS:` / `OPEN_ISSUES:` self-counters from the protocol prompts (kept as sanity-signal; future spec can deprecate once drift is rare).
- Cross-phase ledger queries (e.g. "which Phase 2 claim hardened into a Phase 4 issue?"). Phase 2 and Phase 4 ledgers are independent.

## [0.40.0] — 2026-05-17

### Fixed / Added

- **Critique data integrity — parser coverage, badge wiring, modal load paths, count reconciliation** ([spec 0042](specs/0042-critique-data-integrity.md)) — seven defects whose common thread is "the UI shows a number or a section that doesn't match what the underlying transcript actually contains." **(1) Parser now recognises Phase 1 sections.** `extract_review_items` matches `## N. Claims I Expect the Other Agent Might Dispute` (extracts as `kind="claim"`) and `## N. Open Questions` (extracts as `kind="question"`); leading numeric prefix optional. A new bold-heading detector `**Q1: …**` complements the existing `**C-1**` / numbered-list detectors so Phase 1's question style is picked up. **(2) New `Claim` dataclass + `Run.claims` array on the wire.** Mirrors `Question` shape minus answer-linkage fields. New `ui/claims.py::reconstruct_claims` walks Phase 1 draft files + Phase 2 R1 turn files in one pass; IDs are `Cl-{initial}-p1-{idx}` for Phase 1 entries and `Cl-{initial}-r{round}-{idx}` for Phase 2 R1 difference entries. **(3) `## Diff vs … Phase 1` re-bucketed from `disagreement` to `claim`.** Semantic correction — R1 enumerates contested points being *raised*, only R≥2's `## Substantive disagreements I'm holding` holds them. Claude turn 1 now reports `6 questions · 10 claims · r1` instead of `6 questions · negotiating · r1`. **(4) Aggregator walks Phase 1 drafts.** `_read_phase_review_items` extends from `(2, 4)` to `(1, 2, 4)` with Phase 1's bucket key dropping the round component (`phase1_<agent>`); the no-round form is keyed under the same dict. Phase 1 review-items anchor against the brief (the agent's only input at that point). **(5) Empty buckets always created.** The pre-spec `if not items: continue` skip is removed — an empty list now means "we looked and parsed nothing"; a missing key now means "the turn file doesn't exist." Resolves a class of "right pane is empty but timeline says there were items" mysteries. **(6) `Run.current_draft_path` populated by `load_run_snapshot`.** Surfaces `_find_current_draft_path` on the wire so the Phase 4 full-view modal can resolve its left pane (was previously computed but unused). **(7) Timeline `StatsChips` reads from parsed-item arrays, not agent self-counters.** Per-phase chip allowlist: P0/P3/P5 = none; P1 = claims + questions; P2 = questions + disagreements + claims; P4 = issues + comments + disagreements. The `disagreements` tag can no longer appear on a Phase 1 card. Counts come from `run.{questions,disagreements,claims,issues,comments}` filtered by `raisedTurnKey`; agent self-counters become a sanity-check (logged at debug when mismatched). Falls back to legacy self-counter when `run` + `turnKey` aren't available. **(8) `priorContentPathFor` / `reviewItemsFor` handle Phase 1.** Side-by-side modal can now resolve a left-pane file (the brief) and a right-pane bucket (the new `phase1_<agent>` key) for Phase 1 plan-draft modals. **(9) Critique header `totalIntroduced` reconciles with `open + resolved`.** Was a global cross-phase sum; now uses the existing per-phase `introduced` variable so the header math reconciles within the selected tab. **(10) Markdown renderer setext-heading bug fixed.** Brief content like `**Purpose:** prose paragraph.\n---\n## 1. Origin` was rendering as `<h2><strong>Purpose:</strong> ... prose ...</h2>` because CommonMark setext headings use `---` immediately following a paragraph as an H2 underline. New `_padSetextDividers` pre-pass in `shared.jsx::Markdown` inserts blank lines around bare `---` / `===` dividers so they parse as `<hr>` thematic breaks instead of swallowing the prior paragraph. Same fix eliminates the "first paragraph renders all-bold" symptom. **(11) Side-by-side modal gains a Claims group on the right pane.** When `Diff vs … Phase 1` items extract as `kind="claim"`, they now have their own labelled group in `NegotiateReviewModal` alongside Open Questions / Disagreements / Resolved. **9 new tests** across `tests/ui/test_claims.py` (Phase 1 extraction, no-numeric-prefix tolerance, R1 difference inventory, R≥2 doesn't produce claims, no-section returns empty) and `tests/protocol/test_extract_review_items.py` (Phase 1 claims section, Phase 1 Open Questions without "for X" suffix, Diff vs is claim not disagreement, R≥2 Substantive disagreements still kind="disagreement"). 619 total green. Wire-format additions (`Run.claims`, `kind="claim"`) lay the foundation for [spec 0043](specs/0043-…)'s cross-round ledger; spec 0042 itself does NOT touch protocol prompts, orchestrator state, or convergence logic — that's the explicit boundary.

## [0.39.0] — 2026-05-16

### Fixed / Added

- **Critique classification + load-time resilience + sentiment paragraph + tighter cards** ([spec 0041](specs/0041-critique-classification-and-resilience.md)) — four issues from a single review pass over the Phase 4 / critique surface. **(1) Phase 4 conflated issues, comments, and questions under one "questions" bucket.** Real semantic bug. The protocol uses three distinct sections — `## Open questions for {other}` (questions one reviewer asks the other), `## Issue ledger (delta + currently open)` (items the reviewer found wrong with the *draft itself*, stateful and self-managing), and `## Comments on the current draft` (non-blocking commentary) — but `protocol/parse.py::extract_review_items` pulled items from all three and classified every entry as `kind="question"` (with the comment "Classify as question so the UI groups them"). `reconstruct_questions` then tried to thread answer linkage with a cross-round positional-match heuristic that was designed for actual questions. Two visible consequences on the partner-vetting run: the timeline showed `0 issues` + `APPROVED` (the protocol's terminal state for a successful review) while the critique pane showed `74 Q · 61 open` — three sections combined, none of which were real Q&A linkages. Spec 0041 splits the parser to emit `kind="issue"` / `"comment"` / `"question"` distinctly. New `Issue` and `Comment` dataclasses on `Run.issues` / `Run.comments`. New `ui/issues.py::reconstruct_issues` reads the agent's own `open`/`resolved`/`non-blocking` markers inside each Issue ledger entry as the canonical status — matching the agent's `OPEN_ISSUES: N` end-of-turn counter that the timeline chip already used. The parser also gains a bold-heading entry detector (`**C-1** — ...`) since Claude's ledger uses heading-style entries instead of numbered lists. Partner-vetting Phase 4 now correctly reports `30 I (30 resolved · 0 open) · 0 Q · 0 D · 33 C` — reconciling with the timeline's `0 issues` + `APPROVED`. **(2) Transient `HTTP 502` flash on initial run load fixed.** `useLiveRun` no longer stamps `error` on the first non-2xx response. Internal counter tolerates 3 consecutive failures before surfacing the error screen; once `run` is non-null a transient failure NEVER overwrites the data with the error screen (existing connected indicator dims naturally). First-load retries are paced at 1s / 2s / 4s before settling into 5s steady-state polling — so a cold-start Fly machine wake + Supabase materialise blip doesn't flash an error screen at all. **(3) Question / disagreement / issue / comment cards hard-truncate body at ~70 chars** before the CSS ellipsis kicks in. The column reads cleaner as a scannable list; the full body lives in the expanded surface and on the `title` attribute for hover. **(4) `composeSentiment` expanded to every phase with an overall sentiment lead.** Pre-spec Phase 0/1/3/5 returned `''` and Phase 2/4 produced terse two-sentence output. Now: `**Positive —** Claude approved the draft this round. No open issues this round.` / `**Cautious —** Claude's round-1 difference inventory. Raised 6 new questions. Standing: 6 open questions.` / `**Critical —** Claude did not approve; 5 open issues.` / `**Solid —** Claude delivered an independent Phase 1 draft.` / `**Done —** Final document emitted by Claude.` Bold-rendered inline via a small `renderInlineBold` helper. **(5) `CritiqueExplorer` renders four typed groups.** Type filter becomes `All / Issues / Questions / Disagreements / Comments` with per-kind pills (`I` warn, `Q` info, `D` warn, `C` neutral). Phase tab counts show `30 I · 0 Q · 0 D · 33 C` (zeros collapse). Summary tab table gains `I raised / I resolved / I still open` + `C noted` columns alongside the existing Q + D columns. **6 new tests** in `tests/ui/test_issues.py` (marker-wins-across-rounds, Claude bold-heading parsing, non-blocking ⇒ resolved, ID-token dedup across rewordings) + `tests/ui/test_questions.py` (Phase 4 issue-only turn produces zero questions). 610 total green.

## [0.38.0] — 2026-05-16

### Fixed / Added

- **Critique rework — Phase 4 answer linkage fix + compact cards + Summary tab + Timeline tabs re-alignment** ([spec 0040](specs/0040-critique-rework.md)) — six items from a single user pass over the critique side of the run-detail view. **(1) Phase 4 questions all rendered as `open` regardless of answer state.** Real bug. The protocol uses two different section headings — Phase 2 uses `## Answers to {other}'s open questions`, Phase 4 uses `## Answers to {other}'s prior comments` (see `protocol/prompts.py:591`). The regex at `ui/questions.py:69-72` only matched the Phase 2 form, so every Phase 4 question stayed `open` and the partner-vetting run's Phase 4 tab read `74 Q · 0 D · 0 resolved` even though 13 of those questions had been answered in the very next round. Fix: regex now accepts both phrasings. Partner-vetting Phase 4 now reports `13 answered / 61 open` correctly; the cross-axis click-to-highlight wires to both `raisedTurnKey` and `answeredTurnKey` on every answered question. **(2) Compact question / disagreement cards.** Both `QuestionCard` and `DisagreementCard` collapsed to a single-line headline by default — type pill, round range, one-line truncated body, status pill, chevron. Click to expand reveals the full body, quote / after anchors, and (for questions) the answer body or (for disagreements) the progression timeline + current positions + resolution. Disagreement card collapsed height drops from ~70px to ~38px. Toggling the type filter (`All / Questions / Disagreements`) resets all cards to collapsed. **(3) Click always flashes the timeline endpoints.** Was wired since spec 0034 but only worked reliably for disagreements; with (1) fixed, answered Phase 4 questions now carry `answeredTurnKey` so the click flashes BOTH the raising and the answering turn-card on the left pane. **(4) Timeline plan/turn chip row gains an explicit `↩ N` annotation** when a turn closed prior-round questions or disagreements. Previously the round-over-round delta showed `(-2)`, which read as a generic "two less open" — the new glyph makes the answer-this-round count legible at a glance, with a tooltip surfacing the breakdown. **(5) Summary tab in `CritiqueExplorer`** joins `Phase 2 | Phase 4` once the run reaches a terminal state (`completed`, `deadlocked`, `errored`). Body: per-phase table laid out as one row per round with columns `[Round]` `[Q raised]` `[Q answered]` `[Q still open]` `[D raised]` `[D resolved]` `[D still open]`. Rounds with zero activity are omitted. Aggregate counts in the section header (`13 answered · 61 open · 10 resolved · 0 open`) give the headline shape immediately. Hidden while the run is still in-flight — the numbers shift each poll and the value is the post-mortem overview. **(6) `Conversation / Consumption` tabs moved to the LEFT edge of the Timeline pane toolbar.** Spec 0038 left them stranded against the GPT pill on the right; spec 0040 puts them directly under the "Timeline · N artifacts" title in the PaneHeader where a reviewer naturally looks. New toolbar order: `[Conversation | Consumption] [live-count] [flex] [GPT pill]`. The GPT pill stays right-aligned, preserving the spec 0038 D14 vertical column with the Claude pill on row 1. **2 new tests** in `tests/ui/test_questions.py` covering Phase 4 `'s prior comments` heading recognition + Phase 2 `'s open questions` regression guard. 604 total green. No backend data-model changes; the Phase 4 fix is a 6-character regex edit, the rest is pure UI consumption of existing wire shapes.

## [0.37.0] — 2026-05-16

### Fixed

- **Cost-pipeline integrity — preserve metrics on resume, price the cache tier that was actually used, fold tool spend into the headline** ([spec 0039](specs/0039-cost-pipeline-integrity.md)) — audit of the partner-vetting run (2026-05-16) surfaced a roughly 4× under-report in the user-facing total (`metrics.json` said $2.45 against a recomputed ~$9.86). Three independent defects composed. **(1) Resume lost prior metrics.** `orchestrator/run.py:110` instantiated a fresh `Metrics()` on every session entry — no rehydrate from disk — so the final `metrics.save()` overwrote the pre-resume call list with only the resume-window calls. The aggregator's fallback at `ui/aggregator.py:211-215` was wired backwards: it only kicked in when metrics.json reported `total_cost_usd == 0.0`, so a non-zero resume-window total silently masked the rest of the run. New `Metrics.load_or_new(path)` rehydrates on every session entry; D3 inverts the aggregator's priority so the transcript is now the primary truth and metrics.json is the cold-start fallback. **(2) Cache writes priced at the 5-minute rate when the agent requested 1h.** `anthropic_agent.py:30` has requested `cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral", "ttl": "1h"}` (via the `extended-cache-ttl-2025-04-11` beta) since spec 0017, but `pricing.py:31` hardcoded `cache_write_per_mtok = 3.75` — the 1.25× = 5-minute rate. Anthropic bills 1h writes at 2× input. `ModelPricing` now splits `cache_write_5m_per_mtok` (1.25× input) and `cache_write_1h_per_mtok` (2× input); `TokenUsage` gains `cache_write_5m_tokens` + `cache_write_1h_tokens` (the aggregate `cache_write_tokens` stays as the sum for back-compat on the transcript wire shape); the Anthropic agent reads `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` / `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` and prices each tier exactly. New run multi-round phases now bill correctly — old transcripts can't be retroactively re-attributed because they only carried the aggregate field. **(3) Web-search fees missing from every persisted total.** Spec 0031 D7 had carved `search_cost` out as a side-channel surfaced only in the Consumption tab's expanded panel. With (1) and (2) fixed, the gap between the headline and the invoice became obvious. New canonical `compute_full_cost(model_id, usage, searches)` is what stamps every persisted `cost_usd` (AgentResult, TurnEnded, CallRecord, metrics.json total, RunCompleted, Supabase `runs.total_cost_usd`); `compute_token_cost` is the renamed-but-preserved per-token helper, used for the breakdown ("of which tokens / of which web search"). `TurnEnded` event shape gains `cache_write_5m_tokens` / `cache_write_1h_tokens` / `search_cost`. `TurnTokenUsage` gains `token_cost` (= cost − search_cost, invariant). The Consumption tab relabels its "tool cost" line to "of which web search" — clear that it's a breakdown, not a separate charge. **(4) Transcript dedup by label.** Parse-error recoveries replay the same `turn_ended` label multiple times; the partner-vetting run had every `phase4-r*-*` label appearing twice (the naive transcript sum was $12.74; the deduped sum is $8.78). `load_run_snapshot` pre-filters the event stream to keep only the last occurrence per label; `_sum_transcript_cost` and the recompute tool follow the same convention. Sibling labels (`phase4-r1-claude` and `phase4-r1-claude-repair`) are NOT deduped — they're distinct API calls billed separately, and the agent-level rollup includes both. **(5) New `dual-research recompute-costs --run RUN_ID | --all [--push] [--dry-run]` subcommand.** Re-walks each run's transcript, recomputes every per-turn cost under the new pricing rules (folds search in; old transcripts get 5m attribution for cache writes since the per-TTL split was never recorded), rewrites `metrics.json`, and backs up the original to `metrics.json.pre-0039.bak` on the first invocation (idempotent — re-runs preserve the original-original). All existing local runs backfilled; `runs.total_cost_usd` on Supabase will be updated post-deploy via `recompute-costs --all --push`. **(6) UI breakdown.** `CostBadge` tooltip shows `${cost} (tokens $X · web search $Y)` when `searchCost > 0`. The CLI `[run]` summary line gains the same breakdown when search fees fired. **Note: totals on the run list & detail header will INCREASE for runs that used web search or 1h-cache writes** — the partner-vetting run's headline jumped from $2.45 to $9.86. **22 new tests** across pricing (per-TTL splits, `compute_full_cost` invariants, snapshot table), persistence (CallRecord shape, `Metrics.load_or_new` rehydrate + missing file + corrupt JSON + old-shape tolerance), aggregator (transcript-primary fallback, dedup by label, search-fee fold-in for pre-spec events), and `tests/audit/test_recompute.py` (dedup, idempotence, backup-once, dry-run, `--all` walks directory). 602 total green.

## [0.35.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Web search audit UI + agent-pill alignment fix** ([spec 0038](specs/0038-web-search-audit-ui.md)) — spec 0036 captured per-turn web-search audit data and persisted it to `session_dir/searches/<turn-key>.json`, but the UI didn't yet read any of it. Spec 0038 surfaces every signal the data layer captured, so a human reviewer can finally verify "did the model really have all the necessary inputs as it moved through the pipeline" by reading the queries the model fired, the retrieved URL list (with `page_age` on Anthropic + `[cited]` tags on each cited result), and the `cited_text` snippets the model attributed to each citation — alongside an explicit warning when a citation references a URL that wasn't in any retrieval set. **(1) Per-turn search chip on collapsed `ArtifactCard`s.** Every card whose turn fired web search now carries a `🔎 N` chip in the existing chip row, alongside the token/cost chips — quiet on turns where search didn't happen, prepends `⚠` when a hallucination flag fires. **(2) Per-turn gist on expanded cards.** Below the existing sentiment paragraph, a one-line affordance — `Pulled M results across N queries · click to inspect →` — handles the click into the full-view modal pre-positioned to the Web Search tab. Provider-aware copy: OpenAI without sources renders `M citations · click to inspect →` instead. **(3) `Web Search` tab on every full-view modal.** One-pane modals (Phase 0 critique, Phase 3 doc, Phase 5 final) join `Content | Input | Web Search`; the Phase 2/4 side-by-side `NegotiateLeftPane` gains a `Web Search` sub-tab alongside `Original | Input`; the Phase 1 `DraftReviewModal`'s right pane gains a `Draft | Web Search` sub-tab strip so the draft turn's audit sits next to the draft itself. Each tab body renders a per-query accordion (`QueryGroup`) of `ConsultedSourceCard`s — Anthropic carries title + host + `page_age` chip + monospace `cited_text` block per citation; OpenAI carries URL/host only. The Phase 0 shared `input` card deliberately does NOT gain a Web Search tab (no model call on the brief itself). **(4) Hallucinated-citation warning in three places.** Tab strip badge (`⚠` next to "Web Search" when `flags.cited_url_not_in_consulted_sources` fires), per-query `QueryGroup` header dot when a citation pinned to that event isn't in its retrieval set, and a `HallucinationBanner` at the top of the tab body listing the offending URLs + titles. The validator already populated `Citation.matched_query_id`; the UI just reads it. No "fix this" affordance — the audit is read-only; a reviewer's job is to notice and decide, not to edit. **(5) Run-header `RunSearchSummary` chip.** Sits in the existing chip row alongside cost/status: `🔎 N · M URLs` (total searches across all turns · total consulted URLs); appends `· ⚠ K unmatched` when any turn flags hallucinated citations. Click jumps the timeline to the first flagged card (or the first card with searches if no warnings); hidden entirely when the run has no audit data — pre-0036 transcripts gracefully render nothing. **(6) Server `?include=summary` extension.** The existing `/api/runs/<id>/searches/index` endpoint gains an optional `?include=summary` query param that adds a per-key map of `{queries, consulted, has_warning}` so the chip layer reads from a single per-run fetch instead of one bundle fetch per card. Implemented for both fs and Supabase backends; backward-compatible (default response shape unchanged). New `useSearchIndex(runId)` + `useSearchBundle(turnKey)` hooks in `live-data.jsx` mirror spec 0033's `useInputBundle` byte-for-byte; the run-scoped index lives on a new `SearchIndexContext` so every card + the header summary share one network call. **(7) GPT pill alignment fix (spec-0035 follow-up).** Claude's per-agent pill sits on the right of the Timeline `PaneHeader` (row 1) and GPT's used to sit on the **left** of the `PaneToolbar` (row 2) — the user asked for both right-aligned so they form a vertical column. Pure JSX reorder of the toolbar children: `[live-count chip] [flex] [Conversation | Consumption tabs] [AgentStrip gpt]`. No CSS additions. **(8) Empty-state copy is provider-aware AND replay-safe.** Three honest states: no bundle on disk (pre-0036 or web search disabled), bundle present but no tool events (search enabled but not used), OpenAI-style empty `consulted_sources` (provider returned no retrieval list). Each reads as deliberate rather than as a missing-feature glitch. **(9) `cited_text` is never truncated, never collapsed.** When Anthropic populated `cited_text`, the citation card renders it verbatim in a monospace block — the whole audit value is the human reader being able to read what the model claims it extracted. **(10) 8 new tests** across `tests/ui/test_server.py` (the `?include=summary` query param adds a per-key summary map with `queries` / `consulted` / `has_warning` shape; the existing index path is byte-equal when the param is absent) and `tests/ui/test_search_summary_helper.py` (helper unit coverage: count math, hallucination-flag passthrough, missing-field tolerance, summary skips unreadable JSON, returns empty when no `searches/` dir exists). 580 total green; pre-0036 transcripts hit the no-bundle empty state cleanly with no chip/gist/header artefacts. No data-model changes, no event changes — pure UI consumption of spec 0036's persisted bundles plus the small server-side `?include=summary` extension. **Deferred:** server-side re-fetch of cited URLs (would close OpenAI's snippet gap and hedge link rot, but introduces network/extractor/ToS surfaces — wait until the gap bites in real reviews) and Notion-as-MCP for agents (runtime tool, different audit axis); both tracked as next-spec candidates.

## [0.34.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Web search audit foundation + protocol parser fixes + resume hardening + `--notion` repeatable** ([spec 0036](specs/0036-web-search-audit-foundation.md)) — closes the audit gap surfaced after the first full prod-tier run, plus six round-2 readiness fixes that touch the same parser + orchestrator surface. The unifying gap: when web search fires, dual-research only recorded an integer count — the queries, retrieved URLs, retrieved snippets, and per-citation `cited_text` were all read by the model and then discarded, so a human reviewer had no way to verify the model's claims against ground truth. **(1) Per-turn web-search audit capture.** New `src/dual_research/audit/` package with provider-neutral schema (`TurnSearchAudit` / `ToolEvent` / `ConsultedSource` / `Citation` / `TurnSearchFlags`), per-provider normalisers (`normalize_anthropic_search_audit` walks `web_search_tool_result` blocks + citation annotations; `normalize_openai_search_audit` walks `web_search_call` items + `url_citation` annotations), and a validator (`validate_search_audit`) that cross-references each citation URL against the consulted-source set and stamps four flags (`search_performed`, `cited_url_not_in_consulted_sources`, `citations_without_search_event`, `queries_missing_from_actions`). URL normalisation strips `utm_source=openai` and other UTM-style tracking params before set membership so OpenAI's appended-tracking-param URLs match clean Anthropic retrieval-set URLs. **(2) OpenAI now forces `search_context_size: "high"` + `include: ["web_search_call.action.sources"]` on every web-search call.** Closes the bulk of the "model hallucinates citations even though search ran" failure mode the OpenAI community thread documents — `high` gives the model more page content before generating, and `include` surfaces the full retrieval URL list (without it, only cited URLs come back). **(3) New `TurnSearches` event + on-disk persistence.** The orchestrator's `run_one_call` emits `TurnSearches` right after the agent returns, carrying the normalised audit dict. The aggregator's new `_on_turn_searches` handler runs `validate_search_audit` and writes the bundle to `session_dir/searches/<turn-key>.json` (parallel to spec 0033's `inputs/`), then stamps `TurnTokenUsage.search_audit_path`. New `audit_context` kwarg on `AgentCall.run` carries phase / agent / label / turn_key into each agent so the captured audit knows where it belongs. **(4) Two new HTTP endpoints.** `GET /api/runs/<id>/searches/index` lists the available turn-keys; `GET /api/runs/<id>/searches/<turn_key>` returns the persisted JSON verbatim. Both camel (`phase2Round3Claude`) and snake (`phase2_round3_claude`) key forms accepted. Implemented for both fs and Supabase backends. **(5) Three CRITICAL parser fixes in `protocol/parse.py`.** `EVIDENCE_CHECKED_SECTION_RE` and `CARRYOVER_AUDIT_SECTION_RE` gain `\b` word-boundary anchors so headings like `## Evidence checked this round (3 sources)` or `## Evidence checked this round:` no longer get missed (while still rejecting `## Evidence checked this roundup`). `extract_revised_draft` strips horizontal-rule-only bodies (`----` / `___` / `***`) — the old behaviour treated those as a valid draft and overwrote the converged document. New `extract_revised_draft_inclusive` (used by Phase 4) walks past stray `## Plan summary`-shape siblings the drafter sometimes emits instead of nested `### …` sub-sections, terminating only at a heading in the protocol allowlist. **(6) Three HIGH/MEDIUM orchestrator-hardening fixes.** `emit_final` + `confidence_tag` + `render_metadata_header` now tolerate `phase2_outcome=None` — resuming a run that errored during Phase 2 used to crash at finalisation; the metadata header now renders a "replayed from prior run — Phase 2 details unavailable" note instead. Repair-turn `max_output_tokens` raised from 6144 → 16384 (matches regular turn budget) via a new `REPAIR_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` constant in `repair.py`, also threaded through the Phase 2 hash-drift repair call. Phase 4 gains a resume-aware round skip: at the top of each round, if both `round-NN-claude.md` and `round-NN-openai.md` already exist, the orchestrator replays state from disk (detects revised draft via the new inclusive extractor, checks approval, advances `draft_round`) without issuing API calls or re-emitting events. **(7) `--notion` is now repeatable and combines with `--brief`/`--prompt`.** Argparse uses `action="append"`; `--resume` and `--push` stay mutually exclusive with input sources via explicit validation in `main()`. `build_brief` in `ingest/__init__.py` was rewritten to compose multi-source briefs in CLI order (brief → notion roots in order → prompt) with `\n\n---\n\n# Source: <descriptor>\n\n` dividers between adjacent sources. Single-source invocations are byte-identical to pre-0036 behaviour. **(8) Pre-existing AppVersionChip hooks-order crash fixed inline.** Spec 0035 shipped `AppVersionChip` with a `useState` call *after* the `if (!meta?.version) return null;` guard — a Rules-of-Hooks violation that crashed the chrome bar on every page load until `useAppMeta` populated (i.e. always on first paint). Moved `useState` above the early return. Discovered during VERSION_NOTES verification on this spec; small enough to land here rather than spin a separate hotfix. **(9) 55 new tests** across `tests/audit/` (schema round-trip, two normalisers, six validator rules incl. URL normalisation), `tests/events/`, `tests/ui/` (aggregator persistence + search-audit-path preservation through `TurnEnded`, server endpoints with both camel/snake key forms), `tests/protocol/` (regex word-boundary + HR-stripping + inclusive-draft sibling tolerance), `tests/orchestrator/` (None-guard end-to-end, repair budget constant, phase 4 resume skip vs normal-run), `tests/cli/` (argparse repeat + multi-source concat). 572 total green; `searchAuditPath` lands as `null` on pre-0036 transcripts (replay-safe). UI surfacing of the captured audit (search-count chip on cards, "Web Search" tab in modals, hallucination warning chips, agent-header alignment fix) deferred to spec 0037; this spec ships the data layer + behind-the-scenes fixes.

## [0.33.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Consumption rework + header-placement fix + app-version chip** ([spec 0035](specs/0035-consumption-rework.md)) — three concerns folded into one PR to avoid two run+verify cycles. **(1) Consumption tab — data-relative bar scale.** Bars used to be sized to the model's full 1M-token context window; real per-turn input runs 5–80K so the bar filled barely 1–8% and was unreadable. New `computeConsumptionScale(rows, run)` picks a shared denominator (max observed input × 1.15) across every bar on the grid. The context-window cap renders as a vertical tick marker + label (`1M` / `400K` / `200K`) at its position on the data-scaled bar — both signals (consumption + budget) stay visible. A caption above the grid names the scale: `scale: 87K · cap 1M`. **(2) Per-agent expanded cards with stacked sub-bars.** Click a row to expand. The previous expanded body (one 3-column table of numbers spanning both lanes) is replaced by two side-by-side `ConsumptionCard`s — Claude on the left, GPT on the right. Each card has agent identity in the header, the total bar in agent color, then one sub-bar per input piece beneath at the same scale. Sub-bars sort by descending size by default; a `sort: ↓ size / ↘ order` toggle flips to canonical Tk order. Pieces with zero token count collapse to a single `not used in this turn: …` footnote using friendly labels (the `INPUT_PIECE_LABEL` map from spec 0033). Web-search count + tool cost from spec 0031 survive at the bottom of each card. **(3) `SUBINPUT_COLORS` palette.** The sub-bars use a neutral palette (indigo / ochre / sage / plum / rose / teal / slate-amber) explicitly disambiguous from agent-amber / agent-green — the total in agent color reads as the aggregate, the sub-bars in neutrals read as the components. The collapsed-row segmented bar keeps the existing `KIND_COLORS` palette (still mirrors the how-it-works Tk chips). **(4) App-version chip in the chrome bar.** New `useAppMeta` hook fetches `/api/health` once on mount and caches on `window.__appMeta`. New `AppVersionChip` renders `v{version}` in the chrome bar's right cluster (left of `How it works`); clicking navigates to the how-it-works page so the user can see what landed. Both fs-mode + supabase-mode endpoints already returned `version` — no backend changes. **(5) Header-placement fix (spec-0033 follow-up).** The four-row run header from spec 0033 over-claimed real estate; user feedback redirected. Run header reverts to two rows: topic + cost + status on row 1, phase dots + dot labels + metadata on row 2. The Conversation / Consumption tabs (prominent variant — accent underline on active, font + padding bump) move from the run header back into the Timeline `PaneToolbar`. The two per-agent activity pills — same payload as the spec-0033 strips, just reshaped as inline-flex pills — move from the run header into the Timeline pane: Claude on the right of the `PaneHeader` (same row as `Timeline · N artifacts`), GPT on the left of the `PaneToolbar` (replacing the deleted `AgentLegendChip`s). Single source of truth for per-agent state in the chrome — no more duplicate badges. `Timeline` re-owns its `tab` state internally, unwinding spec-0033's state lift. No new backend tests required (517 unchanged + green); all JSX files parse.

## [0.32.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Critique navigation — first-class Q+D, side-by-side rework, sentiment cards, click-to-highlight** ([spec 0034](specs/0034-critique-navigation.md)) — spec 0033 made the per-turn input legible; spec 0034 makes the critique flow between turns navigable. Five gaps from the first end-to-end run, all sharing one piece of infrastructure: **stable IDs on every question and disagreement, with turn-key references for where they were raised and resolved.** Splitting the spec would have orphaned the data model for a release. **(1) First-class Question objects parallel to Disagreement.** New `Question` dataclass in `ui/models.py` with the same shape — `id`, `phase`, `raised_round`, `raised_by`, `answered_round` / `answered_by` / `answered_turn_key` (nullable), `status` (`open` / `answered`), `body`, `quote` / `after` anchors, `block_id`, `raised_turn_key`. New `ui/questions.py::reconstruct_questions` walks every turn file, assigns IDs in `Q-{raiser_initial}-r{round}-{idx}` shape (e.g. `Q-c-r1-01`), and threads answer linkage by positional match against round R+1's `## Answers to {other}'s open questions` section. The match is annotated `'verbatim'` when the answer contains the question's content tokens, else `'positional'`. No prompt changes — agents emit the same protocol artefacts they already do. **(2) Pre-resolved anchor IDs.** New `protocol/blocks.py::assign_block_ids` segments markdown into blocks (paragraph / heading / list-item / fenced-code / blockquote), assigns sequential `b-N` IDs, embeds `<!-- block-id: b-N -->` HTML comments after each block, and returns the rewritten markdown + a list of `BlockRecord` for parser-time anchor resolution. New `protocol/parse.py::resolve_review_items` walks each turn's review items and stamps `ReviewItem.block_id` by matching the agent's `> quote: …` / `> after: …` anchors against the prior content's BlockRecords (whitespace + case-insensitive). The frontend's `jumpToItem` uses `document.getElementById(blockId)` first — fastest + most reliable — and falls back to the legacy text-scan only when `block_id` is null. Server's `/files/{path}` injects block-id comments into every `.md` served so the rendered DOM carries the same IDs the aggregator resolved against. **(3) Turn-key threading on Disagreement.** New `raised_turn_key` / `closed_turn_key` populated from the disagreement's first/last `ProgressionStep`. Format matches spec 0033's `item.turnKey` (`phase2_round3_claude`). Powers cross-axis navigation — clicking a card in the explorer flashes the matching timeline cards. **(4) DisagreementExplorer → CritiqueExplorer.** Renamed, renders BOTH Qs and Ds typed (`Q` / `D` left-rail tags), per-phase tabs now show `Phase 2 · 3 Q · 4 D`-shape counts, new filter strip (`All / Questions / Disagreements`) on the right of the toolbar. Click any card → corresponding turn-cards in the timeline flash for 2s (`COLORS.info` ring for Qs, `COLORS.warn` for Ds). New `RunDetail`-level `highlightedTurnKeys` Map is threaded down to every `ArtifactCard`. **(5) Sentiment paragraph + chip deltas on unfolded cards.** New `composeSentiment` synthesises 2-3 sentences from `TurnStats` + filtered `run.questions` / `run.disagreements` — `"Claude still negotiating in round 3. Raised 1 new question, answered 3 prior questions, resolved 1 disagreement. Standing: 2 open disagreements · 1 final-surfaced."` Replaces the single-line `composeGist` on Phase 2 / Phase 4 cards. `StatsChips` gains a `prevStats` prop; chip annotations now show `4 Q (-2)` (two answered since last round) / `5 D (+1, -2)` (one new this round, two resolved). The chip tooltip explains. **(6) DraftReviewModal for Phase 1.** Phase 1 plan cards used to open the one-pane `DocumentModal`. They now open a side-by-side modal: brief on the left (with the spec-0033 `Original | Input` sub-tabs), draft on the right. Each draft section heading grows a `🔗 brief` affordance that scans the brief for the closest matching block on click — heuristic substring match, no protocol change. **36 new tests** covering block-ID assignment per markdown shape, anchor pre-resolution (verbatim / paraphrased / heading / case-insensitive), `reconstruct_questions` for round-1 + positional answers + verbatim match confirmation + partial-answer round + Phase 4 path, aggregator integration (`Run.questions` populates, `raised_turn_key` threaded from progression, `block_id` resolved against prior content), server wire format (`questions` camelCased, `phaseReviewItems[*].blockId` round-trips). 517 total green.

## [0.31.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Inputs foundation — universal Input view, Phase 0 split, two-row live header** ([spec 0033](specs/0033-inputs-foundation-and-header.md)) — the first end-to-end test run of the spec-0032 pipeline made the per-turn input opaque: you could see how much went in (Consumption tab) and what came out (timeline), but never *what the model was actually shown*. Spec 0033 ships the data plumbing for that, the per-card UI that surfaces it, and two adjacent UX gaps that shared the "make the input legible" theme. **(1) Per-turn input bundles.** New `TurnInputs` event carries the full Tk-keyed text (`system` + `brief` + `d1` + `d2` + `plan` + `hist` + `draft` + `histp`) for every agent call. Built by new `*_input_bundle()` siblings in `protocol/prompts.py` — each calls its corresponding `*_prompt()` with `_placeholder("<label>")` substituted for inline content, so the `system` text is byte-equal to the prompt template with no possible drift. The orchestrator emits `TurnInputs` right after `TurnStarted` for every dispatch (Phase 0/1/2/3/4 + repair turns + the spec-0032 force-verbatim-copy repair). **(2) On-disk persistence + REST endpoint.** The aggregator's new `_on_turn_inputs` handler writes each bundle to `session_dir/inputs/<turn-key>.json` and stamps `TurnTokenUsage.input_path` so the wire signals which turns have bundles available. Bundles are NOT pushed over SSE — payload is too large; the UI fetches on demand via `GET /api/runs/<id>/inputs/<key>` (accepts both `phase2Round3Claude` and `phase2_round3_claude` forms). A second endpoint `/api/runs/<id>/inputs/index` lists available keys so the UI can detect pre-0033 runs without probing. The Phase 0 shared input bundle is synthesised on-demand from `brief.md` via `build_phase0_input_bundle` if no persisted bundle exists — works for old runs immediately. **(3) Universal Input tab.** Every full-view modal grew an Input tab that lists each piece as an independently-collapsible section (`system` collapsed by default; substantive content open). One-pane modals (Phase 1 plan, Phase 3 doc, Phase 5 final, Phase 0 critiques) carry it as a sibling of Content; the Phase 2/4 `NegotiateReviewModal`'s left pane gains an `Original | Input` sub-tab strip — same `InputTabContent` component. **(4) Phase 0 split into three cards.** The single `input` Phase-0 card became three: shared `input` (brief + system, opens `InputBriefModal`) plus per-agent `p0-claude` / `p0-gpt` critique cards (open `PreflightResponseModal`). The previous single-card view conflated input-with-response; the split is what the user wanted from the start. **(5) Two-row live header.** `RunDetailHeader` went from 2 rows (topic strip + metadata) to 4: topic + cost + status + Conversation/Consumption tabs on row 1; per-agent Claude / GPT strips on rows 2-3 carrying `[icon] [model] · [tokens·cost] │ [status dot + 3-6-word activity sentence]`; phase dots + dot-labels + metadata on row 4. New `composeAgentActivity(agent, run)` is a deterministic switch over `(agent.status × run.phase × run.round)` producing live phrases like `drafting parallel plan` / `negotiating · round 3` / `waiting for Claude`. The `Conversation`/`Consumption` tabs hoisted out of the timeline toolbar into the header right edge, prominent variant with an accent underline on the active tab. **(6) Pre-existing round-label regex bug fixed.** `_round_index_from_label` only matched the `round-N` form, but the orchestrator emits `r{N}-` (e.g. `phase2-r3-claude`). Consumption-tab keys for Phase 2/4 rounds were silently collapsing across rounds in production. The helper now matches both forms; no test regression because the prior tests used the legacy form. 18 new tests covering: input-bundle key parity per phase (the cross-check that prevents sizes-vs-text drift), `TurnInputs` event shape + round-trip, aggregator persistence + stamp on `TurnTokenUsage.input_path`, Phase 0 synthesis from `brief.md`, server endpoint contract (camel + snake key forms, 404 on missing, Phase 0 synth), `inputPath` round-trip through `_to_camel`, label-regex parity for both shapes. 481 total green.

## [0.30.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added / Fixed

- **Phase-2 hash-drift escape, P2 summaries, live-push flag, dual-research-run skill** ([spec 0032](specs/0032-convergence-and-live-push.md)) — four concerns bundled out of the first end-to-end test run. **(1) Phase-2 hash-drift escape (bug fix).** The web-components-catalogue test run looped from round 4 through round 8 of Phase 2 with no convergence: both agents emitted `STATUS: AGREED` / matching drafter / OQ=0 / BD=0 / matching FSD count, but the `AGREED_PLAN` block hashes kept drifting because OpenAI paraphrased the canonical plan each round instead of copying Claude's verbatim. The protocol prompt acknowledges this case ("Same-round AGREED with mismatched plans is a parse error and triggers a repair turn") but the orchestrator never implemented that handler. Spec 0032 adds a new convergence helper `all_substantive_gates_pass_except_plan_hash` and a new `force_verbatim_copy_prompt`: when drift is detected for the first time, the orchestrator fires a repair turn that hands the non-drafter the canonical plan and demands byte-for-byte reproduction. If the repair still drifts, the orchestrator promotes the drafter's plan as canonical (new `DrafterCanonicalPromoted` event), marks Phase 2 converged via `via_canonical_promotion=True`, and proceeds to Phase 3 — substance agreed, wording drift is not a deadlock. **(2) Phase 2 timeline cards no longer empty (bug fix).** `negotiation_round1_prompt` and `negotiation_turn_prompt` both gain a required `## Summary` section, matching the convention already used by Phase 0 / 1 / 3 / 4 prompts. `extract_summary` picks this up automatically, populating `phase_summaries["phase2_round{R}_<agent>"]` so the inline-unfold body added by spec 0030 finally has a TL;DR to display. **(3) `--push-while-running` CLI flag.** When set, the orchestrator spawns an `asyncio.create_task` that calls `RemoteSession.push_session_dir` every 30s in parallel with the run. Failures during the periodic push are logged but do not abort the run; a final synchronous push fires in the run's `finally` block so the hosted UI always sees the completed state, even on errored runs. Requires `SUPABASE_URL` / `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` / `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in env (loaded eagerly at CLI parse time so the flag fails fast with a clear error if any are missing). Opt-in; existing flows unchanged. **(4) `.claude/skills/dual-research-run/SKILL.md`.** Project-local Claude Code skill documenting the canonical recipe for firing a test run: source env keys from `~/.zshrc` via the eval-grep pattern, ensure the local UI server is up on port 6173, fire the run with `--models prod --push-while-running`, report both the local and hosted run URLs, then stay quiet. Versions with the code so the recipe stays in sync with the actual flag names. 20 new tests covering `PlanHashDrift` detection (matched / mismatched drafters, negotiating side, drafter-is-gpt case, empty default), `force_verbatim_copy_prompt` shape (inlines canonical plan, names recipient + drafter, forbids paraphrasing, requires all standard P2 sections, warns about canonical-promotion fallback, pre-fills the STATUS block), `## Summary` section presence in both Phase 2 prompts, and the `_push_watch_loop` lifecycle (periodic ticks, error swallow, stop-event triggers final push, init-failure doesn't crash). 444 total green.

## [0.29.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added

- **Consumption-tab follow-ups — tier-lookup window, click-to-expand bars, per-phase web-search count** ([spec 0031](specs/0031-consumption-followups.md)) — three concerns from first-pass testing of the spec-0030 Consumption tab. **(1) 1M context window for old runs.** Spec 0030's `RunStarted.{agent}_context_window` fields fixed fresh runs only — older transcripts replayed at 200K / 128K because they didn't carry the field. The aggregator now derives the right window server-side by scanning `config.TIERS` for any `ModelSpec` whose `model_id` matches the run's recorded agent model. `claude-sonnet-4-6` → `PROD_TIER.claude.context_window` → 1,000,000. Every existing prod-tier run renders at 1M immediately on the next deploy with zero data migration. Same fallback applied per-turn for defence in depth (handles the theoretical edge where `run_started` was never seen). **(2) Click-to-expand bars.** `ConsumptionRow` is now clickable; clicking toggles an expanded body below it that lays out the per-piece breakdown as an explicit 3-column table — kind label · Claude tokens · OpenAI tokens — with renormalised numbers matching what each bar's segments depict. Rows summarising input total, output, web searches, and tool cost sit beneath. The legend gains a hint: *click any phase row to see exact per-input numbers*. Multiple rows can be expanded independently; click again to collapse. **(3) Per-phase web-search count + cost.** Originally pitched as "tokens used by tools" — but neither provider reports tool tokens separately (search results fold into `input_tokens`). The honest substitute is the per-turn search-request count, which `AgentResult.extras["searches"]` already captured but no event threaded forward. `TurnEnded.searches` now carries it; `TurnTokenUsage` gains `searches` + `search_cost` (computed via a new `ModelPricing.web_search_per_request` rate — $0.010/req for Claude, $0.025/req for the GPT-5 family). Search cost is a separate side-channel — does NOT join `compute_cost` (which stays strictly token-cost), so the headline `cost` chip keeps its existing semantics. The expanded row shows web searches + tool cost per lane only when at least one lane had a search in that turn, avoiding clutter for non-search phases. 18 new tests covering pricing rates + `compute_search_cost`, aggregator wiring for `searches` / `search_cost`, the TIERS lookup fallback (with unknown-model fall-through), and wire-format presence of all new fields. 424 total green.

## [0.28.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added

- **Timeline UX pass — inline unfold, per-input consumption segments, real context windows, parser repairs** ([spec 0030](specs/0030-timeline-ux-pass.md)) — five UI / data follow-ups to spec 0029 packed into one pass. **(1) Inline unfold.** Clicking a timeline card no longer opens the modal directly. Instead the card unfolds in place to show a synthesised gist line (e.g. `Claude still negotiating, raised 4 questions, conceded a held D-N.`), the `## Summary` TL;DR, and a new **View in full mode** button — that button is now the only entry into `ArtifactModal`. Live items skip the toggle (they still stream inline). **(2 + 3) Disagreement parser fallback.** `_parse_one_entry` in `ui/disagreements.py` gained a heading-only fallback: when no `(a) D-N: "…"` sub-bullet exists, it walks the block, skips meta prefixes (`status:`, `> quote:`, `> after:`, `- > quote:`), and takes the first content line as `point` (capped at 200 chars with `…` truncation). The same pass picks up `**I claim:**` / `**They claim:**` bullets for `my_position` / `other_position`. The Disagreements panel now renders an actual contested-point sentence; progression timeline entries show "raised <point>" instead of bare "raised". **(4) Per-input consumption segments.** The Consumption-tab bars now decompose into one coloured segment per prompt-piece kind in the Tk palette (`brief` / `d1` / `d2` / `plan` / `hist` / `draft` / `histp`) — the same vocabulary as the `ChatLifecycle` chips on the how-it-works page. Each phase orchestrator computes piece sizes from its input strings at call time via a new `protocol/prompt_pieces.py` helper (single char÷3.5 heuristic for both providers; provider's reported `input_tokens` is the cost-of-record, piece widths are renormalised to sum to it). Pieces ride the `TurnEnded` event in a new `prompt_pieces: dict[str, int]` field; aggregator preserves them on `TurnTokenUsage.prompt_pieces`. **(5) Real context windows.** `agents/context_windows.py` (introduced in spec 0029 with hand-rolled values) is **deleted**. `RunStarted` now carries `claude_context_window` and `openai_context_window`, sourced from the tier's `ModelSpec.context_window` (1M for `PROD_TIER`; 200K / 400K for `TEST_TIER`). The aggregator stamps them on `AgentState.context_window` and on each `TurnTokenUsage.context_window`; the frontend reads the bar denominator straight off the wire — prod-tier runs now display 1M-token-wide bars for both lanes. 25 new tests (prompt-pieces shapes per phase + renormalise + zero-history edge cases; aggregator wiring for both `context_window` and `prompt_pieces`; wire-format roundtrip including camelised inner keys; disagreement parser fallback for heading-only / bullet-meta / label-echo / inline-precedence cases). 406 total green.

## [0.27.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added

- **Token-consumption tab — per-turn context-window visualisation** ([spec 0029](specs/0029-token-consumption-tab.md)) — the timeline pane on a run-detail view now carries a two-pill segmented control on the left of its toolbar: `Conversation` (existing card view, unchanged) and `Consumption` (new). The two per-agent total chips that used to sit at the left of the toolbar move to the right end. Switching to `Consumption` swaps the artifact-card list out for a 3-column grid — phase / round label · Claude lane · OpenAI lane — borrowed verbatim from the `ChatLifecycle` visual on the how-it-works page. Each row is **one API call** (one turn per round per agent); P0 / P1 / P3 give a single row each, Phase 2 and Phase 4 give one row per round per agent, so the visual makes the buildup explicit — round 3 of Phase 2 is visibly larger than round 1 because the conversation history grew. Each bar is sized to its model's **actual** context window (Claude 200K, GPT-5.5 200K, GPT-5-mini 128K, default 128K for unknown ids) sourced from a new `dual_research/agents/context_windows.py` registry; the percent number under each bar gives the apples-to-apples comparison when the two lanes have different denominators. Bars have three layers: cache-read portion (lighter shade, ≤ input), fresh input (full agent colour), and a thinner output tail (darker shade, 60% height). Hover any bar for the full breakdown (input · cache read · output · cache write · cost · model id). The new `TurnTokenUsage` dataclass + `Run.phase_token_usage` flat dict captures the per-turn detail in the aggregator from `TurnEnded` events (which already carried `input_tokens` / `output_tokens` / `cache_read_tokens` / `cache_write_tokens` / `cost_usd` / `model_id` — the data was being discarded before this spec). Keys use the existing `phase{N}_<agent>` / `phase{N}_round{R}_<agent>` convention that `phase_summaries` and `phase_review_items` already use; the wire-format camelizer rewrites them to `phase{N}{Agent}` / `phase{N}Round{R}{Agent}` (e.g. `phase2Round1Claude`). Runs replayed from pre-0029 transcripts have an empty dict; the Consumption tab shows a small "no per-turn data" empty state. 17 new tests (per-phase key shapes, payload roundtrip, accumulation preservation, registry coverage, wire-format inner-key + field-name camelisation). 389 total green.

## [0.26.0] — 2026-05-16

### Added

- **Cross-review inline comments — Phase 4 side-by-side modal** ([spec 0028](specs/0028-review-inline-comments.md)) — final spec on the visualisation track. Clicking a Phase 4 turn card now opens the same side-by-side modal as Phase 2: left pane is the **current converged document**, right pane is the agent's issues / comments / disagreements as anchored cards. Click a card → left pane scrolls + flashes; `> after:` cards mount a dashed "insert here" ghost block.
